## Where the register stands

Counted on 30 October 2026 from data/entries.csv, which holds 329 entries.

Definitions used for every count on this page. A promise is an entry that sets a test. A scoring entry records a result against an earlier promise. Open: live, not scored, scoring date on or after the count date. Due: live, not scored, scoring date before the count date.

- **Entries:** 329 = 304 promises + 25 scoring entries.
- **Scored PASS:** 9 (AB1, AB1-OFFICIAL, BoC, C39-CTTE, DLV-C15, DLV-CTR, DLV-DEN, MTL-LABEL, TR-FTA-ROUND).

- **Open:** 271.
- **Due:** 0.
- **More than 14 days past due:** 0. New promises can be added.

- **Proposed, not yet live:** 2 (KAMLOOPS-EXCAV, QC-SEATMATH).

## Possible overlaps under review

These pairs of entries may test the same thing. Both entries in each pair are kept and stay live while the overlap is under review. Nothing has been removed or changed in either entry.

- **LEVELS-1NOV and LEVELS2729** (noted 22 Oct 2026): Both record the tabling of the 2027 to 2029 Immigration Levels Plan under IRPA s.94 with a first check on 2 Nov 2026. LEVELS-1NOV records TABLED (date and URL) or NOT TABLED with the 30 sitting day window; LEVELS2729 records the tabling date and whether the permanent resident target rises above 380,000. LEVELS-1NOV's own text says it is not a substitute for LEVELS2729. Both kept, under review, nothing removed.
- **FA3-MVH-DEC and FA3** (noted 22 Oct 2026): Both test a Schedule 1 listing or a dated refusal for MVH. FA3-MVH-DEC tests a listing Order (P.C. number and date) or a dated public refusal on or before 2 Nov 2026; FA3 tests MVH and RBT2 listed or refused in Schedule 1 by 31 Dec 2026. FA3-MVH-DEC's own text says it does not move the FA3 31 Dec row. Both kept, under review, nothing removed.

The Register

Entries as at 30 October 2026. Last change: 30 October 2026.

This page is a citizen-held register of Canadian public commitments. Each line is a test: what counts as a pass, the date it is scored and who answers for it. The main table holds files owned by Canadian governments, legislatures, courts, regulators, Crown bodies and companies, and foreign decisions a Canadian file depends on. My own commitments, and outside files I watch, sit in short sections of their own under the same rules.

The page is dull on purpose. It does not argue, campaign or ask for anything. The essays make the arguments; this page keeps the score.

Nothing here is edited quietly. Every change is a dated entry in a public record that keeps its full history, at https://github.com/citizenheldrecord/register/commits/main. If this page and that record ever differ, the record governs.

The owner on each line is my designation of who answers for the outcome. It is not a finding of fact about any person or body.

The rules

Every entry has three things: the outcome that counts as a pass, one fixed scoring date and a named owner.

On its scoring date an entry is scored PASS or FAILED. "On track" and "progressing" are not outcomes.

A FAILED entry is published within 30 days of the date. It names the layer that blocked it: title, courts, regulation, financing, statute, political choice, or another layer named in full. Its cost is priced with a source for every number, or marked OPEN.

No silent extensions. A new date is a new entry that cites the old one.

The record is permanent and append only for substance: no test, owner, date, result or source is ever edited or deleted. Internal working notes may be removed under rule entries R-2026-10-24-A and R-2026-10-25-A (from a test, internal approval notes only), each removal logged with the row's fingerprint before and after. A reversal or a correction goes in as a new, dated entry.

Resolved entries get three sub-scores, hype, means and cost, each MATCHED, MIXED, FAILED or OPEN. They never change the PASS or FAILED.

Every entry carries a cost to date, filled in on the scoring date from a public source showing the figures of whoever holds them (department, Crown body, company, court, foreign government, the Bank of Canada or this desk), cited with URL and date. If no public figure exists on that day, the cost sub-score is FAILED and names whoever holds the figure. "Cannot determine" is FAILED. No exemptions. The desk never estimates it. It sits inside the cost sub-score and never changes the PASS or FAILED.

Every entry carries a fingerprint, the SHA-256 checksum of its canonical text on the day it goes in, listed in a manifest kept in the public record and snapshotted on the Wayback Machine at each update. A new date, and a scoring, are each a new dated entry citing the prior entry's id and fingerprint, never an edit in place. How to recompute it, and the script, are in the rule entry of 18 October 2026.

Fingerprints use layout chr-entry-v2 from 18 October 2026, which adds the hype, means and cost sub-scores. The v1 manifest is kept and the v2 manifest is published alongside it (rule entry C).

Rules apply from their entry date forward and are never retroactive. Entries scored on or before 18 October 2026, including HR5334, keep cost to date OPEN.

Each line says why its date is what it is (a statute, a court file, a government promise, a scheduled release, a company timetable, or a reason I set and state), where the result will be read, the budget line where one is documented, and the essay that set the test. Anything not yet found is marked OPEN.

Each line also says how the money behind it is set. CASELOAD-COUPLED: the budget line grows with the volume of the problem. FIXED-OUTCOME: the money is tied to a named deliverable or milestone. TIME-LIMITED: the money sunsets on a printed end date. OPEN: no primary page shows any of these. A line is marked coupled, fixed or time limited only when a Main Estimates, Supplementary Estimates, Public Accounts, contribution agreement or Canada Infrastructure Bank financing page (or a provincial Estimates page) shows it, and that page is cited in the Money column. Everything else is OPEN, and the Money column still carries the line and amount where one was found.

Some files cannot be scored PASS or FAILED on one fixed date as written: they record a fact, a status or a condition, or carry a reading or a scoring day the main table does not use. They sit in their own section as dated records and are not counted in the main table.

Every update prints the SHA-256 checksum of the full table as a CSV file, and a Wayback Machine snapshot is taken of this page and the record. Anyone who downloads the CSV can check it against the printed checksum.

On 3 January each year the register states the files: every entry closed, open or failed, owners named, with the checksum of the whole register.

The register's only product is the score. No advocacy, no grants, no policy shop.

Using this page

The table can be filtered by section, status and owner, and searched by any word. The whole table downloads as CSV or JSON, with nothing hidden by the filters.

Checksum for this update: SHA-256 of public-live-rows-2026-10-30d.csv (all 329 rows) is 37ad2c5ee2e68161a728628a66cfa0ee8beee8d8b9057fa3d66eb783242e7b73

Rule entries

R-2026-10-18-A. Entry fingerprint

Entered 18 October 2026. From this date every entry carries a fingerprint: the SHA-256 checksum of its canonical text, taken on the day the entry goes in.

The canonical text is built the same way every time, so anyone can recompute it. The first line is the tag chr-entry-v1. Then come fifteen lines, one per field, in this order: id, section, scoring_date, sort_date, test, owner, date_source, status, measure, money, funding, argument, cost_to_date, supersedes_id, supersedes_hash. Each line is the field name, an equals sign, then the value. A field with no value is left empty after the equals sign. In each value, the text is normalised to Unicode NFC, every run of spaces, tabs or line breaks becomes one space, and spaces at the start and end are removed. The lines are joined by a single line feed, with no line feed at the end, and encoded as UTF-8. The fingerprint is the SHA-256 of those bytes, written as 64 lowercase letters and digits.

A new date is a new entry. It gets its own id and fingerprint and cites the old entry's id and fingerprint in supersedes_id and supersedes_hash. The old entry stays, with its old fingerprint.

Scoring is also a new entry. On the scoring date the result goes in as a new, dated entry with its own id and fingerprint, citing the scored entry's id and fingerprint in supersedes_id and supersedes_hash. The scored entry is not edited in place.

Every update publishes a manifest listing each entry's id and fingerprint, with the checksum of the CSV it was computed from. The manifest is kept in the public record and a Wayback Machine snapshot of it is taken at each update. Manifests are never replaced: each update adds a new one.

If an entry's fingerprint ever differs from the last manifest without a dated entry explaining why, that is itself a correction to be entered. The script that computes the fingerprints is published in the record at scripts/hash_entries.py, and anyone can run it on the downloaded CSV. The public record's commit history is already a chain of checksums; the fingerprints let a reader check a single entry without trusting this page.

This rule applies from 18 October 2026 forward. Entries scored on or before that date, including HR5334, stand as recorded.

R-2026-10-18-B. Cost to date

Entered 18 October 2026. Every entry carries a cost to date. It is filled in on the scoring date from a public source that shows the figures of whoever holds them: the department, Crown body, company, court, foreign government, the Bank of Canada or this desk. For governments that means sources such as the Public Accounts, a Departmental Results Report, the Main or Supplementary Estimates, a Parliamentary Budget Officer report, a written answer or Order Paper response, or an access to information release; for others, their own published reports, filings or records. The cell gives the amount, the source, its URL and the date it was read.

If no public figure exists on the scoring date, the cost sub-score is FAILED, and the entry names whoever holds the figure. "Cannot determine" is FAILED. There are no exemptions. The desk never estimates a cost to date. Before the scoring date, the cell reads OPEN.

Cost to date sits inside the existing cost sub-score. Like hype and means, it never changes the PASS or FAILED and never moves a cell.

This rule applies from 18 October 2026 forward and is never applied retroactively. Entries already on the register carry cost to date OPEN until their own scoring date. Entries scored on or before 18 October 2026, including HR5334, keep cost to date OPEN.

R-2026-10-18-C. Fingerprint layout version 2

Entered 18 October 2026. From this date the fingerprint uses layout chr-entry-v2. It is built exactly as the chr-entry-v1 layout in rule A, except that the first line is the tag chr-entry-v2 and three more lines follow the fifteen fields of v1, in this order: hype_score, means_score, cost_score. Every entry now carries all three sub-scores, each MATCHED, MIXED, FAILED or OPEN. An entry not yet resolved reads OPEN.

The reason for the change: the v1 layout left the hype, means and cost sub-scores outside the fingerprint, so a sub-score could have changed without any fingerprint showing it. Layout v2 closes that gap. Any column added to the register later needs a new layout and a dated rule entry; until then, that column is outside the fingerprint and the rule entry says so.

The v1 manifest of 18 October 2026 is kept unchanged in the record. From this update a v2 manifest is published alongside it, named entry-hashes-v2 followed by the update, and every build checks the data against both. No v1 fingerprint changes. The v2 fingerprints are new numbers for the same entries, not corrections.

A second update on the same day carries a letter after the date, as in 2026-10-18b, so no published file is ever overwritten.

R-2026-10-19-A. A capacity test on every levels plan

Entered 19 October 2026. Each time an Immigration Levels Plan falls due under section 94(1) of the Immigration and Refugee Protection Act, the register enters one capacity test for that plan before it is tabled. PASS: any permanent resident target above the prior plan's target for the same year is made expressly conditional on at least one published, numeric capacity measure with a stated trigger (housing starts or completions against new residents, a dated corridor or financing milestone, or a productivity or investment series). PASS-NO-INCREASE: no increase, test not triggered. FAILED: an increase with no such condition. The entry sits in the section for recorded files, because it carries a third reading and its scoring day can move to within 3 days of tabling, with a hard stop at the statutory window. The next plan's entry goes in the day after the current one is scored. A companion reading of housing completions per additional resident may be entered; its baseline is filled only from a primary source and reads OPEN until then.

R-2026-10-19-B. Transfer files: delivered against committed

Entered 19 October 2026. The register may hold entries for single international assistance transfers, read from the department's own project data. Each is scored on a fixed date on three points: disbursed at or above committed, activity status closed or in finalisation, and a published result linked to the activity. Any one missing is FAILED. Limits: at most five transfer entries open at any time; only activities with a committed amount of CAD 50 million or more and a published planned end date. The figures are the department's own and are labelled as such; the register does not present them as an audit. A loss or fraud finding abroad is never attached to a Canadian file unless a source traces it to Canadian funds. No totals, rankings or commentary on recipients.

R-2026-10-19-C. Ratchet hold

Entered 19 October 2026. An entry that scores a reduction (a headcount, a spending share, a backlog) may carry a conditional hold entry: the lower level is still met on the same series three readings later. The hold is scored only if its parent is scored PASS. If the parent is FAILED, the hold is entered VOID on the same day, not extended. Holds sit in the section for recorded files, beside the register's other conditional entries.

R-2026-10-19-D. Sunsets that fire

Entered 19 October 2026. A statutory sunset, or a statutory review deadline, in a statute already tied to an entry on this register may be entered and scored on its own date. PASS: on that date the consolidated text shows the limit unamended and the power has lapsed, or the review report has been laid. FAILED: the limit was extended or removed, or the review was not laid, cited by bill number or Journals entry. This scores the statute's own date as written. It complements the entry that scores whether a new power carries a sunset at all (BOOMBUST-SUNSET). Statutes not tied to a register entry are not added.

R-2026-10-19-E. Instrument tag

Entered 19 October 2026. Where an entry involves public money, the money column begins with INSTRUMENT: followed by GRANT, LOAN, GUARANTEE, EQUITY or IN-KIND, set only when a primary page shows it, and otherwise INSTRUMENT: OPEN. For a loan or a guarantee, the cost to date on the scoring date gives the outstanding or guaranteed amount, from the Public Accounts of Canada or the lender's own published page, separately from any grant amount. The desk never estimates it.

Labels and the fingerprint. Rules A, B and D also put EVIDENCE-HOLDER: SELF (the owner's own data) or EVIDENCE-HOLDER: INDEPENDENT (a body other than the owner) at the start of the measure column. These labels, and the INSTRUMENT tag, are written inside columns that already exist (test, measure, money, date source), so they are inside the chr-entry-v2 fingerprint. No column is added by these rules, so no new fingerprint layout is needed and chr-entry-v2 stays in use. A column added later still needs its own layout and rule entry (rule entry C of 18 October 2026).

These rules apply from 19 October 2026 forward and are never retroactive. No existing entry is changed.

R-2026-10-19-F. Next on the clock

Entered 19 October 2026. The page shows, above the table of entries, the five nearest live entries not yet scored, on or after the date of the update, in scoring-date order, with scoring date, id, section and owner, and one line naming every live entry past its scoring date and not yet scored. It is display only and is rebuilt with each update, so it reads as at the update date. No column is added and no entry is changed; the chr-entry-v2 fingerprint is unaffected.

R-2026-10-19-G. Snapshot check

Entered 19 October 2026. After each update the page, the dated CSV and the dated manifest are saved to the Wayback Machine, and scripts/check_wayback.py checks that every recorded snapshot answers with its archived date. A missing or unresolved snapshot is reported, not hidden; where an update has none, the register says so in its failure or correction notes rather than leaving the gap silent.

R-2026-10-19-H. Cap guard on transfer entries

Entered 19 October 2026. The limit of five open transfer entries in rule entry B of 19 October 2026, and its CAD 50 million floor, can be changed only by a new dated public rule entry that states the old limit, the new limit and the reason. No transfer entry beyond the limit in force is entered before that rule entry is published. scripts/add_entry.py refuses a sixth open transfer entry (ids beginning XFER-), and a pipeline test proves it.

R-2026-10-19-I. Third mirror

Entered 19 October 2026. A third public copy of this repository is planned on Codeberg (codeberg.org), as a pull mirror of the GitHub repository, so the record survives the loss of any one host. Status: PENDING SETUP. The mirror address is added to this page by a later dated rule entry once it is live and its CSV checksum matches this copy.

R-2026-10-19-J. Third copy on Codeberg

Entered 19 October 2026. The third public copy named in rule entry I of 19 October 2026 is at https://codeberg.org/citizen-held-record/register. Status: LIVE (manual copy, refreshed by the desk after each update). It is a copy, not an automatic mirror: after each update the desk refreshes it and checks that its head commit and the checksum of the dated CSV match this repository. If they do not match, the register says so in its correction notes and names the update the copy last matched.

These rules apply from 19 October 2026 forward and are never retroactive. No existing entry is changed.

R-2026-10-22-A. Reading aids and essay links (display only)

Entered 22 October 2026. The page shows three display-only aids, rebuilt with each update from the table: a status summary at the top (counts by status and section, entries past their scoring date and not yet scored, and entries due in the next 30 days), a box on how to read the page with one worked example, and a list of the essays that set tests. The essay column shows a link to the published essay where one exists, read from data/essay-links.csv in this record, which maps an entry id to the essay's title, address and publication date. Each row id links to that row. No column is added to the CSV and no entry is changed: a row's own argument field stays as entered, and where the two differ the published essay link is the reading aid and the entry is the record. The essay-links file is outside the chr-entry-v2 fingerprint; changes to it are listed in the public record's commit history. Where an entry as entered shows an internal desk working reference (a working file name or note), the page and README show "[internal desk reference hidden in display, 22 Oct 2026]" in its place; the entry, the CSV and the JSON stay as entered, and each such case is listed in a dated correction entry.

This rule applies from 22 October 2026 forward and is never retroactive. No existing entry is changed.

R-2026-10-24-A. Housekeeping

Entered 24 October 2026. Housekeeping (24 October 2026): internal working notes (approval notes, timestamps, internal file references) are not part of the record and may be removed from any row. Removal never changes a row's outcome, date, owner, test, sources or result, and is shown in the published file fingerprints.

How it is shown. Each removal is listed in data/housekeeping-log.csv with the date, the update, the row id, the field, and the row's fingerprint before and after. The changed row gets a new fingerprint in the next dated manifest, and that manifest names the log and its checksum. The build accepts a changed fingerprint only when the log lists it, and only when the change is in the date source, measure, money, funding or essay field and either deletes text or replaces it with OPEN; any other change still stops the build. Earlier dated files stay as published, so anyone can compare a row before and after. This is the one exception to the rule that an entry is never edited after it goes in.

First use, update 2026-10-24a: rows BM1, KAMLOOPS-EXCAV, EA-REFORM (date source), QC-SEATMATH (essay) and COVID-RESIDUE (measure).

This rule applies from 24 October 2026 forward.

Correction, 25 October 2026. The sentence "This is the one exception to the rule that an entry is never edited after it goes in" is restated: entries are append only for substance; internal working notes may be removed under R-2026-10-24-A, and, from a test, internal approval notes only under R-2026-10-25-A, each removal logged with the row's fingerprint before and after.

R-2026-10-25-A. Housekeeping in the test field

Entered 25 October 2026. Rule entry R-2026-10-24-A is extended to the test field, for one purpose only: removing internal approval notes, such as a note that a change was approved or when it was approved. Nothing else in a test may be removed under this rule. Removal never changes a row's outcome, date, owner, test meaning, sources or result. Where an approval note cannot be taken out without changing what the test says, it stays as published.

How it is shown. As under R-2026-10-24-A, each removal is listed in data/housekeeping-log.csv with the date, the update, the row id, the field, and the row's fingerprint before and after, one line per fingerprint layout. The build accepts a changed test only when the log lists it and the change deletes one stretch of text; a test can never be replaced with OPEN, and any other change still stops the build. Earlier dated files stay as published, so anyone can compare a row before and after.

First use, update 2026-10-25a: row HOUSING-STARTS (test). In the same update, row BEC1 (date source) has an approval note removed under R-2026-10-24-A. The rows before the change are in public-live-rows-2026-10-24c.csv (and, for the v1 layout, public-live-rows-2026-10-18.csv).

This rule applies from 25 October 2026 forward. R-2026-10-24-A stays as entered.

Ways promises get eaten

Four mechanisms, set out in the essay The Reforms Were Eaten: Why the Thermometer Must Be Citizen-Held. Link: OPEN (not yet published).

Capture: the reform is administered by the beast it was meant to close, and becomes one more open file drawing its ration from the unfinished status.

Goodhart: once a measure becomes a target, the machine optimizes the number, and the published figures improve while the outcome does not.

The ratchet: each crisis leaves a higher baseline of spending, staffing and authority, and when the reform wave recedes the ratchet does not.

Time horizons and concentrated interests: those paid by the process are organized and those who pay are diffuse, and the payoff of reform arrives after the next election while the pain arrives before it.

Every entry is a dated test those four have to get past.

This register records from outside the institutions it measures. It does not ask any of them to measure itself.

How the record keeps itself honest

The rule book is written to stop the register's own keeper from cheating. Fingerprints (SHA-256), a published manifest, Wayback snapshots and an append-only rule make silent edits noisy. A new date is a new entry citing the old fingerprint. Cost stays OPEN until the scoring date; on that day it is a public figure with a URL and date, or it is FAILED.

Government promises (147)

Promises with a government owner, a primary source and a scoring date. Each row also names its file section.

Recorded facts and checkpoints | HCAP-LVL | The 2027 to 2029 Immigration Levels Plan conditions any increase in permanent-resident admissions on published capacity measures. PASS = the tabled plan (levels plan, supplementary information or the s.94 annual report) makes any 2027 PR target above 380,000 expressly conditional on at least one published, numeric capacity measure with a stated trigger (housing starts or completions against new residents, a dated corridor or financing milestone, or a productivity or investment series). FAILED = PR target above 380,000 with no such condition; enter dated failure within 30 days (blocking layer, priced cost, political entry). NO INCREASE (2027 target at or below 380,000) = PASS-NO-INCREASE, test not triggered. <!-- AUDIT -->

Note, 27 Oct 2026: This register's rule is that every row rests on a primary source, read against that row. On 27 Oct the desk began a row-by-row audit of that rule. It found that many rows were checked against wire reports such as Reuters rather than against the body that made the decision, and that in a few rows the wording went further than the source. Each row now gets its own sources record, listing the primary, the exact line it supports and the date it was read. A row shows as sourced only when that record backs its whole claim. As of this build: 115 rows are sourced, 0 secondary-sourced, 37 have their promise sourced with the outcome pending, 90 partly sourced and 87 unsourced, and 0 do not yet have a record. These counts will be updated with each build until every row is done. Corrections from the audit are entered as new dated lines, and no score has been changed.

## How to check this update

Build stamp: update 2026-10-30d, counted as at 30 October 2026, from data/entries.csv, 329 rows, SHA-256 f001ec4833252031d3fc00e56504b679fbedc92921b4700f9a48b1231ef3849b. The build stops if this file, its row count or any count on this page does not match.

Entry fingerprints for this update: [manifest/entry-hashes-v2-2026-10-30d.csv](https://github.com/citizenheldrecord/register/blob/main/manifest/entry-hashes-v2-2026-10-30d.csv). Housekeeping log: [data/housekeeping-log.csv](https://github.com/citizenheldrecord/register/blob/main/data/housekeeping-log.csv), SHA-256 172d6cee7f5cb986b66f2b79a113ca4b6b645da6a3dcd231408247d1cc20fc4b, 14 lines.

How to check a housekeeping change. 1. Find the row in data/housekeeping-log.csv and note its layout and the two fingerprints. 2. Download the last dated CSV published before the change (for the changes of 24 October 2026, public-live-rows-2026-10-23c.csv) and run python3 scripts/hash_entries.py public-live-rows-2026-10-23c.csv --show ID to see the row's canonical text; the SHA-256 of that text is the before fingerprint. 3. Do the same on the current CSV for the after fingerprint. 4. Compare the two texts: only the logged field may differ, and only by deleted text or by OPEN.

Append only, stated exactly: entries are append only for substance. Tests, owners, dates, results and sources are never edited or deleted; a new date, a correction or a result is a new dated entry. The exceptions are rule entries R-2026-10-24-A and R-2026-10-25-A: internal working notes may be removed (from a test, internal approval notes only), each removal logged with the row's fingerprint before and after, and earlier dated files stay as published.

How entries get in (dated 25 October 2026). This register is a record of the tests this desk has published, not a census of government promises. An entry exists where an essay, report or desk note, or a Gazette or official-source sweep, set or found a dated, two-outcome test on a named owner; it then goes LIVE on Barry Sharp's go and is never removed. Government promises the desk has not written about are not here, and absence from the register means nothing about a promise. Entries in "Government promises" have a government owner; entries with a desk-set test or a non-government owner sit in "Also watched"; baselines sit in "Recorded". Dates: as at update 2026-10-30d, of 329 entries, 185 (about 56 per cent) carry a benchmark date set by the register, 2 an outside date with a scoring window set by the register, and 142 (about 43 per cent) a date set by an outside source (statute, court, scheduled release, government or company timetable). Every entry's date source is labelled. A desk-set date is a benchmark, not a legal deadline: a FAILED against it means the owner did not deliver by the date we chose and published in advance, nothing more. The share is recounted at each update.

Provenance (rule R-2026-10-26-A, 26 October 2026). Posts and press reports are leads only. No row is scored on them alone: every row must rest on a primary, a publication by the body that made the decision or holds the record. A row whose date, fact or evidence rests only on a news report or post is flagged with a dated replacement task, the primary the desk will look for and the date by which it will look. A primary found later is recorded in a new dated line; the row's own cells are not edited. Flags and replacements are in the CORRECTIONS folder; the first six (C39-CTTE, C39-D1, S2, S7, MTL-CLOSE, NOV1) are in CORRECTIONS/2026-10-26-provenance-six-rows.md.

Limits of this register. A fingerprint or checksum proves a file or row has not changed since it was published. It does not prove the evidence is complete, that the test is fair, or that a date set by the register is the right benchmark. Anyone may challenge any row: open an issue naming the row id and the reason, and the answer goes in as a dated entry.

## How promises are chosen

A promise goes on the register when it has four things:

1. A public commitment by a body with the power to deliver it: a government, legislature, court, regulator, Crown body or company.
2. An outcome that can be checked, written as a dated test with two results, PASS or FAILED.
3. A primary source, the body's own publication or record, where the result will be read.
4. An owner, named by the desk as the one who answers for the outcome. That naming is my designation, not a finding of fact.

Selection is one citizen's judgment. The register is not a census of government promises. A promise that is not here has not been judged; its absence means nothing.

To find candidates, the desk runs a Gazette, LEGISinfo and official-source run every Monday, Wednesday and Saturday: Canada Gazette Part I and Part II, bills on LEGISinfo and official government releases. A weekday morning news-pattern sweep also checks the Gazette.

The selection rule is published as rule R-2026-10-25-E (How entries get in). Every pass condition is written and dated before the result is known, and is never edited afterwards: a new date or correction is a new dated entry.

Other observers may pick different tests, such as environmental conditions, Indigenous consent thresholds or fiscal risk. To propose one for the same promise, open an issue at https://github.com/citizenheldrecord/register/issues naming the row id and the test you would use. The answer goes in as a dated entry.

Append only, in date order. Each entry gives the ID, the original deadline, the date it failed, the original test as written, the layer that blocked it (with its evidence link and actor), the priced cost (a source for every number, or OPEN) and what was chosen instead.

A failed entry is never overwritten. A new promise gets a new ID, which cites the failed entry.
